value : null;\n+}\n+\n+export function getKnownFinalVideoDurationSeconds(\n+  finalVideo: Pick<FinalVideoAssetSource, 'id' | 'location' | 'durationSeconds'>,\n+  assets?: Record<string, AssetRegistryEntry>,\n+): number | null {\n+  const explicitDuration = readPositiveNumber(finalVideo.durationSeconds);\n+  if (explicitDuration !== null) {\n+    return explicitDuration;\n+  }\n+\n+  if (!assets) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  for (const assetEntry of Object.values(assets)) {\n+    if (assetEntry.generationId !== finalVideo.id && assetEntry.file !== finalVideo.location) {\n+      continue;\n+    }\n+\n+    const assetDuration = readPositiveNumber(assetEntry.duration);\n+    if (assetDuration !== null) {\n+      return assetDuration;\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  return null;\n+}\n+\n+export async function resolveFinalVideoDurationSeconds(\n+  finalVideo: Pick<FinalVideoAssetSource, 'id' | 'location' | 'durationSeconds'>,\n+  assets?: Record<string, AssetRegistryEntry>,\n+): Promise<number | null> {\n+  const knownDuration = getKnownFinalVideoDurationSeconds(finalVideo, assets);\n+  if (knownDuration !== null) {\n+    return knownDuration;\n+  }\n+\n+  try {\n+    const metadata = await extractVideoMetadataFromUrl(finalVideo.location);\n+    return readPositiveNumber(metadata.duration_seconds);\n+  } catch {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+}\n+\n+export function buildFinalVideoAssetEntry(\n+  finalVideo: Pick<FinalVideoAssetSource, 'id' | 'location' | 'thumbnailUrl'>,\n+  durationSeconds?: number | null,\n+): AssetRegistryEntry {\n+  return {\n+    file: finalVideo.location,\n+    type: 'video/mp4',\n+    ...(readPositiveNumber(durationSeconds) !== null ? { duration: durationSeconds as number } : {}),\n+    generationId: finalVideo.id,\n+    ...(finalVideo.thumbnailUrl ? { thumbnailUrl: finalVideo.thumbnailUrl } : {}),\n+  };\n+}\n*** End Patch\n"

> AGENT

The bug was in two places.

When you dropped a shot into the editor, the drop path always rebuilt the shot as image clips, even if that shot already had a final video. I changed that in [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts:113), so a dropped shot now prefers its final video immediately, registers it as a video asset, and places a single video clip on the timeline using the real video duration.

The dead space came from the final-video switch logic keeping the old image-span length and just swapping the asset underneath it. That meant a shorter final video would freeze on the last frame. I fixed that in [useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts:32), [shot-group-commands.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.ts:331), and the new [finalVideoAssets.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/finalVideoAssets.ts:42), so the editor now resolves the actual final-video metadata and trims the timeline clip to the source video length instead of leaving a frozen tail.

Verification: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipEditing.test.ts` and `npx eslint ...` on the changed files both passed. I did not run a full app build.

```json
}
```

hmm, there's still empty still time at the end of teh clip when i drop it onto the timeline- why could this be?

> AGENT

I’m tracing the remaining tail from the real final-video records now. The likely gap is that the dropped shot path still falls back to a guessed duration when it can’t read metadata for the stored video URL, so I’m checking what duration data already exists on the final-video rows and whether we’re ignoring it.

> AGENT

The likely failure mode is now pretty specific: `shot_final_videos` already exposes `params`, but the frontend query only fetches `id/location/thumbnail_url/...` and skips `params`, so if the URL metadata probe fails we fall back to a guessed duration. That would produce exactly the frozen tail you’re still seeing. I’m confirming the params shape and then I’ll patch the hook to use stored duration before it ever guesses.

> AGENT

I found the most probable reason. `shot_final_videos` already includes `params`, but `useShotFinalVideos()` does not fetch or parse it, so the editor only knows `location` and ends up probing the video URL for duration. If that probe fails on the real stored URL, we silently fall back to a guessed length and you get the frozen tail. I’m patching the final-video query to carry stored duration through the map so drop/switch stop depending on URL metadata.

> AGENT

The most likely cause was that the drop path still didn’t have a reliable duration for some real final-video records.

`shot_final_videos` already exposes `params`, but `useShotFinalVideos()` was only fetching `id`, `location`, `thumbnail_url`, and `shot_id`. So when you dropped a shot, the editor often had to infer duration by loading the video URL itself. If that metadata probe failed for the stored URL, it fell back to a guessed length, which shows up as a frozen still tail at the end.

I patched that so final-video duration comes from persisted DB params first in [useShotFinalVideos.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useShotFinalVideos.ts:13), with the parser in [finalVideoAssets.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/finalVideoAssets.ts:15). The existing drop and switch logic in [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts:113) and [shot-group-commands.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.ts:331) then uses that real duration instead of a fallback.

Verification passed:
`npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/finalVideoAssets.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipEditing.test.ts`
and `npx eslint` on the changed files.

If you want, I can commit and push this follow-up fix too.

> AGENT

Because the timeline currently treats these as two separate truths:

1. The clip’s timeline span.
2. The source media’s real playable span.

Those are not enforced to match.

Concretely:

- The timeline duration is derived from the clip config itself: `clip.from`, `clip.to`, `clip.speed`, or `hold`.
  See [config-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:18) and [config-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:89).
- The renderer mounts a Remotion `Sequence` for that full timeline duration and then hands the media element `trimBefore`/`trimAfter`.
  See [VisualClip.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/VisualClip.tsx:255).
- Nothing in that render path says “if the actual video file is shorter than this clip, shrink the clip.”
  So if `clip.to` or `action.end` is too long, the clip can outlive the real file and you get a held final frame instead of an automatic cut.

That’s why the dead space is even possible at all: the timeline model is authoritative, and source duration is only a hint during creation, not a hard invariant.

Why it is still happening after the last fixes:

- The `shot_final_videos` view can return the playable `location` from a child generation for single-segment shots, while still preserving the parent generation identity.
  See [20260414093000_expose_variant_fetch_generation_id_on_shot_final_videos.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260414093000_expose_variant_fetch_generation_id_on_shot_final_videos.sql:12).
- In that same view, `location` may come from the child, but `params` still come from `g.params`, which is the parent row.
  See [20260414093000_expose_variant_fetch_generation_id_on_shot_final_videos.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260414093000_expose_variant_fetch_generation_id_on_shot_final_videos.sql:28).
- So we can still be building the dropped timeline clip from parent-side duration data while rendering child-side media.

That mismatch is the most likely remaining cause.

The real fix should be one of these, preferably both:

- Make `shot_final_videos` expose duration for the actual playable media, not just parent params.
  Best version: add child params or a resolved `duration_seconds` column to the view.
- Add a hard guard in the editor so media clips cannot exceed `assetEntry.duration`.
  That means clamping `to`/timeline duration on drop, on switch-to-final-video, and ideally again at render/normalize time.

So the short answer is: it’s possible because the editor allows “clip length” and “file length” to drift apart, and your current final-video lookup can still hand us duration from a different row than the actual video URL.

If you want, I can patch this at the root by changing the Supabase view to return the resolved playable duration and then enforce a media-duration clamp in the editor so this class of bug stops being possible.

> DEVELOPER

is there some poor or complicated structure here?

> AGENT

I’m checking the local structure around the video editor and final-video flow with the `desloppify` skill, focusing on whether this bug exists because the model is too indirect or split across too many layers.

> AGENT

Yes. In this area, the structure is more complicated than it should be, and that complexity is exactly why this bug class is possible.

**Findings**
1. The system has multiple competing sources of truth for duration.
   In [config-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:18), timeline duration comes from clip config: `from`, `to`, `speed`, or `hold`.
   In [finalVideoAssets.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/finalVideoAssets.ts:15), duration is inferred again from final-video params, registry entries, or URL metadata.
   In [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts:113), drop behavior decides what to create.
   In [useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts:32), switching decides duration again.
   That is too many places deciding the same fact.

2. The DB view can mix parent identity/params with child media location.
   In [20260414093000_expose_variant_fetch_generation_id_on_shot_final_videos.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260414093000_expose_variant_fetch_generation_id_on_shot_final_videos.sql:12), `location` may come from a child generation, but `params` still come from `g.params` on the parent row at line 28. That means “playable file” and “reported metadata” can describe different records.

3. The renderer trusts the timeline span more than the source media span.
   In [VisualClip.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/VisualClip.tsx:104), media gets `trimBefore` and `trimAfter`.
   But the actual mounted sequence length comes from [config-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:89) and [VisualClip.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/VisualClip.tsx:268).
   There is no hard invariant that says “a media clip cannot outlive the playable source.” So the last frame can be held.

4. The logic is spread across large, high-coupling files.
   [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts:1) is 503 lines.
   [shot-group-commands.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.ts:1) is 572 lines.
   Those files are doing too many jobs at once: drop interpretation, asset resolution, group mutation, duration shaping, and state repair.

So: not “the whole editor is bad,” but this specific final-video/timeline boundary is structurally weak.

**Why this bug exists**
The bug exists because the architecture allows this invalid state:

- timeline clip says “show 5.0s”
- actual playable video only has 3.8s
- renderer still mounts the clip for 5.0s

Once that state is allowed, frozen-tail bugs are inevitable.

**What a cleaner structure would look like**
1. One canonical duration authority for media clips.
   Prefer the actual playable asset record, not clip config and not mixed parent/child params.

2. `shot_final_videos` should expose resolved playable duration directly.
   Same row, same media, same metadata.

3. A normalization pass should clamp every media clip:
   `timelineDuration <= playableSourceDuration / speed`
   That should happen on drop, on switch-to-final-video, on load/migrate, and ideally before render.

4. The renderer should not be able to display beyond source.
   Even if upstream data is wrong, render-time clamping should prevent frozen tails.

If you want the blunt answer: yes, this area is overly indirect and under-constrained. The main smell is not just “too much code,” it’s “important invariants are implied instead of enforced.”

**Megaplan Result**

Plan: `root-fix-the-video-editor-so-20260414-0318`  
Execution mode: `auto-approve` from local config  
Robustness: `light`  
Current state: `finalized`  
Next step: `execute`, but the harness stalled before batch 1

The light run did complete the planning loop and produced a finalized plan, but `megaplan execute` showed no forward progress after 3 checks on April 14, 2026, so no megaplan-driven code changes were actually executed in that run. The finalized artifacts are [final.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/.megaplan/plans/root-fix-the-video-editor-so-20260414-0318/final.md), [faults.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/.megaplan/plans/root-fix-the-video-editor-so-20260414-0318/faults.json), and [plan_v2.meta.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/.megaplan/plans/root-fix-the-video-editor-so-20260414-0318/plan_v2.meta.json).

**What The Plan Says**

The plan’s core diagnosis is that this is not just one drop bug. There are two structural problems:
- Resize clamping currently protects audio but not visual-track video, so the timeline can still allow a clip span longer than the actual media.
- Final-video duration is still derived from too many places, so the system needs a stronger preferred source for duration metadata.

The finalized plan is:
- Broaden resize clamping from audio-only to all `clipType === 'media'` clips in [useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts), while explicitly not constraining `text` or `effect-layer`.
- Add regression coverage in [useClipResize.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.test.tsx) for video, missing-duration video, text, and effect-layer cases.
- Audit the existing drop path in [useAssetManagement.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAssetManagement.ts) and [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts) because the plan concluded that path is probably already correct when duration metadata is present.
- Add a computed `duration_seconds` column to the `shot_final_videos` view via a new Supabase migration, then make [useShotFinalVideos.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useShotFinalVideos.ts) prefer that column over client-side JSON parsing.
- Regenerate Supabase types and remove temporary casts.
- Add tests for `buildSwitchShotGroupToFinalVideoMutation` in [shot-group-commands.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.test.ts).
- Run the targeted video-editor test suite and a small throwaway verification script for resize limits.

**Important Critique Findings**

The critique phase caught two real plan bugs and corrected them:
- `shot_final_videos` is a view, not a writable table. So the right fix is `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` with a computed duration column, not table mutation or worker write-path changes.
- The resize fix must be guarded by `clipMeta.clipType !== 'media'`, not just “remove the audio-track check,” otherwise text/effect-layer clips on visual tracks would get broken constraints.

One minor open warning remains: the proposed SQL `duration_seconds` extraction is intentionally narrower than the full client-side parser, so the client fallback still needs to stay in place for older/nested payload shapes.

If you want, I can take this finalized light megaplan and execute it manually in the repo now, without relying on the stalled `megaplan execute` phase.

> AGENT

I executed the repo-side part of the plan.

The main behavioral fix is in [useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts:43): resize clamping now applies to any `clipType === 'media'`, not just audio-track clips. That closes the structural hole that allowed visual-track video clips to be stretched past their real media span, which is how frozen tail space stays possible on the timeline. I added regression coverage in [useClipResize.test.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.test.tsx:13) for four cases: clamped visual-track video, unclamped video with unknown duration, unclamped text, and unclamped effect-layer. I also kept and verified the shot-group mutation coverage in [shot-group-commands.test.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.test.ts:340) so switching an image group to final video clamps to known duration and falls back to image-span when duration is missing.

On the final-video duration side, [useShotFinalVideos.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useShotFinalVideos.ts:22) now prefers a `duration_seconds` field when the DB view exposes it, but it uses `select('*')` so the app stays compatible before that migration is applied. It still falls back to the existing client parser from `params`. I also added the DB-side migration at [20260414103000_add_duration_seconds_to_shot_final_videos.sql](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/supabase/migrations/20260414103000_add_duration_seconds_to_shot_final_videos.sql:1), and made it better than the original light plan by computing duration from the same playable source as the URL: if the view is serving the child segment URL, it prefers child-derived duration too. I manually updated [types.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/integrations/supabase/types.ts:1545) so the new field is typed.

I also audited the drop path you were worried about. The flow is coherent there: `resolveFinalVideoDurationSeconds(...)` in [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts:117) feeds `durationSeconds` into `registerGenerationAsset(...)`, which stores it as `duration` in [useAssetManagement.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAssetManagement.ts:312), and `buildAssetDropEdit(...)` then uses that `assetEntry.duration` as the clip’s `to`/timeline length in [useAssetManagement.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAssetManagement.ts:175). So the remaining “deadspace is possible” issue was the resize/clamping gap, plus unreliable duration sourcing when the view didn’t expose it cleanly.

Verification:
- `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.test.tsx`
- `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.test.ts`
- `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/finalVideoAssets.test.ts`
- `npx eslint src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.test.tsx src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useShotFinalVideos.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/shot-group-commands.test.ts`

One blocker remains: `supabase db push --linked` did not apply the new view migration because the linked project already has migration-history drift unrelated to this change. The command reached the remote and then stopped with “Remote migration versions not found in local migrations directory.” I left the frontend compatible with the current DB state by using `select('*')`, so this repo change is safe locally, but the new `duration_seconds` view column is not actually deployed yet. The broader `src/tools/video-editor/` test sweep also has preexisting unrelated failures in `AgentChat.test.tsx` and `VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx` around missing router/AgentChatProvider context; I did not touch those.

If you want, I can do the next step and repair the Supabase migration history so I can actually push this view change to the linked database.

I found the concrete cause for your shot. Its `shot_final_videos` row for `1b5c2eb0-6111-446c-b3cf-409f46628484` is returning `duration_seconds: null`, and the params payload is using `num_frames: 49` with `fps_helpers: 16`, which is about `3.06s`. > AGENT

I found the actual cause for your shot `1b5c2eb0-6111-446c-b3cf-409f46628484`.

That shot’s final-video row was not actually “5 seconds”. It was coming through with `num_frames = 49` and `fps_helpers = 16`, which means about `3.0625s`. The editor/parser did not recognize that payload shape, so it treated the duration as unknown and fell back to the old hardcoded `5` second default at drop time. So no, this was not “the shot stores 5 seconds somewhere” in the intended model; it was a parser gap that made unknown-duration clips default to 5.

I patched both sides:
- Client parser now derives duration from `num_frames + fps_helpers` and related frame-count payloads.
- The `shot_final_videos` view now computes `duration_seconds` for that same payload shape.
- I verified your exact shot now returns `duration_seconds = 3.0625` from Supabase.

This is pushed to GitHub on `main` in commit `a583466c2` (`Fix final video duration parsing for frame-count payloads`).

Important testing note: this only fixes new clip creation. If that shot was already dropped onto a timeline as a 5-second clip, it will stay 5 seconds until you remove it and drop it again, or re-run the switch-to-final-video action after a refresh. Please hard refresh, then re-drop that shot. If it still lands as 5 seconds after a fresh drop, then there is one more path bypassing `useShotFinalVideos`, and I’ll trace that next.

> DEVELOPER

Can you understand where the 'dead time at end of clip' thing comes from and is there any way we should deal with it? e.g. by having it visually distinct and being able to click to open a menu to trim it?

> AGENT

I’m mapping the exact sources of “dead time at end of clip” in the editor code so I can answer this concretely instead of as a UX guess. Then I’ll tell you whether a visible “overhang” affordance makes sense or whether it’s papering over the wrong abstraction.

> AGENT

Yes. In this editor, “dead time at end of clip” is not a separate object. It happens when the clip’s timeline span is longer than the playable media span.

Concretely, there are two durations in play:

- The clip’s timeline duration.
  This comes from `clip.from`, `clip.to`, and `clip.speed`, via [config-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17) and [config-utils.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:25).
- The actual source media duration.
  This lives on the asset entry / final-video metadata and is what resize clamping now uses in [useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResizeGesture.helpers.ts:43).

When those drift apart, Remotion still renders the clip for the full timeline span, and the `<Video>` just runs out of source frames and freezes on the last frame. You can see that in [VisualClip.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/VisualClip.tsx:136) and [VisualClip.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/VisualClip.tsx:213): the component trusts the clip’s duration and passes trim props to the media element, but it does not independently clamp the enclosing sequence to the asset’s real playable length. So “dead time” is really “implicit frozen-tail overhang”.

There are two main ways that overhang gets created:

- On drop, if duration is unknown, the editor still falls back to `5` seconds.
  That is in [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts:117) and [useAssetManagement.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useAssetManagement.ts:198).
- On older clips, the timeline may already contain a too-long span from before duration was resolved correctly.

So yes, I understand where it comes from, and yes, you can deal with it visually, but that should be a safety rail, not the main fix.

What I think the product should do:

- Default behavior: never create overhang for normal video clips.
  If actual duration is known, clamp immediately.
- If duration is unknown at drop time:
  Create the clip in a temporary “duration unresolved” state, not as a fake 5-second normal clip.
- Once real duration resolves:
  Auto-trim the clip down if the user has not intentionally edited it longer.
- If you ever want a longer tail intentionally:
  Model that explicitly as “freeze last frame” or “hold end frame”, not as an overlong media clip.

For UI, a visible distinction is reasonable as a secondary safeguard:

- Show the overhang section with diagonal hatching or a dimmed tail region.
- Put a warning badge on the clip edge when `timelineDuration > playableDuration`.
- Clicking it could open a small menu:
  - `Trim to media end`
  - `Keep frozen tail`
  - `Convert tail to hold clip`
- Tooltip text should say something direct like:
  `Video ends at 3.06s, clip extends to 5.00s`

That said, I would not make this the primary workflow. If normal video clips frequently need manual trimming, the data model is still too loose.

The structurally correct model is:

- Asset/final-video duration is canonical source media duration.
- Clip `from/to/speed` defines which part of that source is used.
- Timeline span is derived from that, and cannot exceed it unless there is an explicit freeze/hold mode.
- “Frozen tail” should be a first-class setting, not an accident.

So my recommendation is:

1. Keep fixing root causes so overhang is rare.
2. Add a visible overhang warning only as a guardrail.
3. If you want creative flexibility, add explicit `hold last frame` rather than allowing accidental dead tail.

If you want, I can implement that next as a concrete feature:
- detect overhang on media clips,
- render the tail with a striped warning region in the timeline,
- and add a one-click `Trim to media end` action.

> AGENT

Plan: `design-a-robust-fix-for-dead-20260414-0414`  
Execution mode: `auto-approve` from config  
Robustness: `light`  
Current state: `finalized`  
Next step: `execute`  
Artifacts: [final.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/design-a-robust-fix-for-dead-20260414-0414/final.md), [plan_v2.meta.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/design-a-robust-fix-for-dead-20260414-0414/plan_v2.meta.json), [faults.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/.megaplan/plans/design-a-robust-fix-for-dead-20260414-0414/faults.json)

The finalized light plan says the right abstraction is “overhang”, not generic dead time: timeline span beyond playable media span. It proposes an `overhang.ts` utility to detect that accurately, including speed-aware math, then uses that to render a striped amber tail region on clips, with a divider where playable media ends. That gives you a visible distinction without pretending the overhang is valid video.

The revised plan explicitly avoids one bad idea: it does not build a background auto-clamp/retry system. The critique caught that there is no real infrastructure for duration backfill after a failed lookup, and there’s no way to distinguish accidental fallback overhang from deliberate user intent. So the plan narrows to:
- do one retry at drop time before falling back
- show confirmed overhang visually
- add explicit actions for `Trim to media end` and `Hold last frame`

Two design details matter:
- Overhang math must account for `speed`, or the warning region and trim amount will be wrong.
- Pinned shot-group clips need a special menu gate change, because today those clips don’t expose the normal clip context menu where trim/hold actions would live.

The plan chooses a pragmatic `Hold last frame` implementation: split into two clips, a trimmed video clip plus a normal `hold` clip for the tail, rather than inventing a new single-clip freeze-tail model. That keeps implementation smaller and reuses existing infrastructure.

If you want, I can execute this finalized plan next.

```json
{
```
```json
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/overhang.ts\n+import { updateClipOrder } from '@/tools/video-editor/lib/coordinate-utils';\n+import { getNextClipId, type ClipMeta, type TimelineData } from '@/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data';\n+import type { TimelineAction } from '@/tools/video-editor/types/timeline-canvas';\n+\n+const MIN_TIMELINE_DURATION_SECONDS = 0.05;\n+const DEFAULT_HOLD_FRAME_RATE = 30;\n+const OVERHANG_EPSILON = 0.0001;\n+\n+export interface ClipOverhang {\n+  sourceDurationSeconds: number;\n+  totalTimelineDurationSeconds: number;\n+  playableTimelineDurationSeconds: number;\n+  overhangTimelineDurationSeconds: number;\n+  overhangSourceDurationSeconds: number;\n+  mediaEndFraction: number;\n+}\n+\n+export interface ClampClipToMediaDurationResult {\n+  nextAction: TimelineAction;\n+  metaPatch: Partial<ClipMeta>;\n+  overhang: ClipOverhang;\n+}\n+\n+export interface ConvertOverhangToHoldResult {\n+  clipId: string;\n+  holdClipId: string;\n+  trackId: string;\n+  rows: TimelineData['rows'];\n+  metaUpdates: Record<string, Partial<ClipMeta>>;\n+  clipOrderOverride: TimelineData['clipOrder'];\n+  overhang: ClipOverhang;\n+}\n+\n+function getSafePositiveNumber(value: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {\n+  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0 ? value : fallback;\n+}\n+\n+function getSafeStart(value: number | undefined): number {\n+  return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.max(0, value) : 0;\n+}\n+\n+export function getMediaEndFraction({\n+  playableTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+  totalTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+}: {\n+  playableTimelineDurationSeconds: number;\n+  totalTimelineDurationSeconds: number;\n+}): number {\n+  if (!Number.isFinite(playableTimelineDurationSeconds) || !Number.isFinite(totalTimelineDurationSeconds) || totalTimelineDurationSeconds <= 0) {\n+    return 1;\n+  }\n+\n+  return Math.min(1, Math.max(0, playableTimelineDurationSeconds / totalTimelineDurationSeconds));\n+}\n+\n+export function detectClipOverhang({\n+  clipMeta,\n+  timelineDurationSeconds,\n+  sourceDurationSeconds,\n+}: {\n+  clipMeta: Pick<ClipMeta, 'from' | 'speed'>;\n+  timelineDurationSeconds: number;\n+  sourceDurationSeconds?: number | null;\n+}): ClipOverhang | null {\n+  const safeSourceDuration = getSafePositiveNumber(sourceDurationSeconds ?? undefined, 0);\n+  if (safeSourceDuration <= 0) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  const totalTimelineDurationSeconds = Math.max(\n+    MIN_TIMELINE_DURATION_SECONDS,\n+    Number.isFinite(timelineDurationSeconds) ? timelineDurationSeconds : 0,\n+  );\n+  const safeFrom = getSafeStart(clipMeta.from);\n+  const safeSpeed = getSafePositiveNumber(clipMeta.speed, 1);\n+  const remainingSourceDurationSeconds = Math.max(0, safeSourceDuration - safeFrom);\n+  const playableTimelineDurationSeconds = Math.max(\n+    MIN_TIMELINE_DURATION_SECONDS,\n+    remainingSourceDurationSeconds / safeSpeed,\n+  );\n+  const overhangTimelineDurationSeconds = totalTimelineDurationSeconds - playableTimelineDurationSeconds;\n+  if (overhangTimelineDurationSeconds <= OVERHANG_EPSILON) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  return {\n+    sourceDurationSeconds: safeSourceDuration,\n+    totalTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+    playableTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+    overhangTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+    overhangSourceDurationSeconds: overhangTimelineDurationSeconds * safeSpeed,\n+    mediaEndFraction: getMediaEndFraction({\n+      playableTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+      totalTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+    }),\n+  };\n+}\n+\n+export function clampClipToMediaDuration({\n+  action,\n+  clipMeta,\n+  sourceDurationSeconds,\n+}: {\n+  action: TimelineAction;\n+  clipMeta: Pick<ClipMeta, 'from' | 'speed'>;\n+  sourceDurationSeconds?: number | null;\n+}): ClampClipToMediaDurationResult | null {\n+  const overhang = detectClipOverhang({\n+    clipMeta,\n+    timelineDurationSeconds: action.end - action.start,\n+    sourceDurationSeconds,\n+  });\n+  if (!overhang) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  const safeFrom = getSafeStart(clipMeta.from);\n+  const safeSpeed = getSafePositiveNumber(clipMeta.speed, 1);\n+\n+  return {\n+    nextAction: {\n+      ...action,\n+      end: action.start + overhang.playableTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+    },\n+    metaPatch: {\n+      to: Math.max(safeFrom, safeFrom + overhang.playableTimelineDurationSeconds * safeSpeed),\n+    },\n+    overhang,\n+  };\n+}\n+\n+export function convertOverhangToHold({\n+  current,\n+  clipId,\n+  sourceDurationSeconds,\n+  frameRate,\n+}: {\n+  current: TimelineData;\n+  clipId: string;\n+  sourceDurationSeconds?: number | null;\n+  frameRate?: number | null;\n+}): ConvertOverhangToHoldResult | null {\n+  const clipMeta = current.meta[clipId];\n+  if (!clipMeta?.asset) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  const sourceRow = current.rows.find((row) => row.actions.some((action) => action.id === clipId));\n+  if (!sourceRow) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  const sourceIndex = sourceRow.actions.findIndex((action) => action.id === clipId);\n+  if (sourceIndex < 0) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  const sourceAction = sourceRow.actions[sourceIndex];\n+  const clamped = clampClipToMediaDuration({\n+    action: sourceAction,\n+    clipMeta,\n+    sourceDurationSeconds,\n+  });\n+  if (!clamped) {\n+    return null;\n+  }\n+\n+  const holdClipId = getNextClipId({\n+    ...current.meta,\n+    '__pending-overhang-hold__': { track: clipMeta.track },\n+  });\n+  const safeFrameRate = getSafePositiveNumber(frameRate ?? undefined, DEFAULT_HOLD_FRAME_RATE);\n+  const frameDurationSeconds = 1 / safeFrameRate;\n+  const holdSourceStart = Math.max(0, clamped.overhang.sourceDurationSeconds - frameDurationSeconds);\n+  const holdAction: TimelineAction = {\n+    id: holdClipId,\n+    start: clamped.nextAction.end,\n+    end: sourceAction.end,\n+    effectId: `effect-${holdClipId}`,\n+  };\n+  const holdMeta: ClipMeta = {\n+    asset: clipMeta.asset,\n+    track: clipMeta.track,\n+    clipType: 'hold',\n+    hold: clamped.overhang.overhangTimelineDurationSeconds,\n+    from: holdSourceStart,\n+    to: clamped.overhang.sourceDurationSeconds,\n+    speed: 1,\n+    volume: 0,\n+    opacity: clipMeta.opacity,\n+    x: clipMeta.x,\n+    y: clipMeta.y,\n+    width: clipMeta.width,\n+    height: clipMeta.height,\n+    cropTop: clipMeta.cropTop,\n+    cropBottom: clipMeta.cropBottom,\n+    cropLeft: clipMeta.cropLeft,\n+    cropRight: clipMeta.cropRight,\n+  };\n+\n+  const rows = current.rows.map((row) => {\n+    if (row.id !== sourceRow.id) {\n+      return row;\n+    }\n+\n+    const actions = row.actions.flatMap((action, index) => {\n+      if (index !== sourceIndex) {\n+        return [action];\n+      }\n+\n+      return [clamped.nextAction, holdAction];\n+    });\n+\n+    return { ...row, actions };\n+  });\n+\n+  const clipOrderOverride = updateClipOrder(current.clipOrder, sourceRow.id, (ids) => {\n+    const insertionIndex = ids.indexOf(clipId);\n+    if (insertionIndex < 0) {\n+      return [...ids, holdClipId];\n+    }\n+\n+    return [...ids.slice(0, insertionIndex + 1), holdClipId, ...ids.slice(insertionIndex + 1)];\n+  });\n+\n+  return {\n+    clipId,\n+    holdClipId,\n+    trackId: sourceRow.id,\n+    rows,\n+    metaUpdates: {\n+      [clipId]: clamped.metaPatch,\n+      [holdClipId]: holdMeta,\n+    },\n+    clipOrderOverride,\n+    overhang: clamped.overhang,\n+  };\n+}\n*** End Patch\n"
```

> AGENT

Implemented and pushed on `main` as `eb6244d2f` (`Make video clip overhang explicit on the timeline`).

What changed:
- New drops now retry final-video duration resolution once before falling back to 5 seconds, so transient metadata misses are less likely to create fake tail time. That change is in [useExternalDrop.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts).
- The timeline now explicitly detects video overhang, draws the frozen tail as a striped amber region, and exposes context-menu actions to `Trim to media end` or `Hold last frame`. That wiring lives in [TimelineEditor.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx), [ClipAction.tsx](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx), and the new [overhang.ts](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/overhang.ts).
- Pinned shot-group clips can now open the menu for these overhang actions even though their normal clip menu stays locked down.

Verified with `npx tsc --noEmit` and targeted Vitest runs covering the new overhang math, clip menu behavior, and external-drop duration retry path. I left unrelated local changes in `src/tools/travel-between-images/...` untouched and out of the commit.
